  "Step 1: To convert the given equation to the standard form of a parabola, we need to isolate x^2 on one side and keep the constant term (if any) on the other side. In this case, we can rewrite the equation as follows:

Now, let's focus on completing the square for the terms involving x.

Step 2: To complete the square for x^2 - 4x, we take half of the coefficient of x (which is -4), square it, and add it to both sides. Half of -4 is -2, and squaring that gives us 4.

x^2 - 4x + 4 = -8y - 12 + 4

Step 3: Now we can rewrite the equation in terms of a perfect square:

(x - 2)^2 = -8y - 8

Step 4: Finally, let's express the equation in vertex form by isolating y and getting rid of the negative sign.

-8y = (x - 2)^2 + 8
y = -(1/8)(x - 2)^2 - 1

Now we have the standard form of a parabola. The vertex is at point (2, -1)."

"Final Answer: y = -(1/8)(x - 2)^2 - 1."

***********************{Explanation:
Step 1: To convert the given equation to the standard form of a circle, we need to complete the square for both x and y terms.

Step 2: The standard form of a circle is (x - h)^2 + (y - k)^2 = r^2, where (h,k) is the center of the circle and r is its radius.

Step 3: First, we rearrange the given equation to group x terms and y terms together:

x^2 - 4x + y^2 + 2y = 11

Step 4: Next, we add and subtract the square of half the coefficient of x (which is (-4/2)^2 = 4) inside the parentheses for the x term. Similarly, we add and subtract the square of half the coefficient of y (which is (2/2)^2 = 1) inside the parentheses for the y term:

x^2 - 4x + 4 + y^2 + 2y + 1 = 11 + 4 + 1

Step 5: This simplifies to:

(x - 2)^2 + (y + 1)^2 = 16

Step 6: Comparing this with the standard form of a circle, we can see that the center is at (h,k) = (2,-1) and the radius is r = √16 = 4.

Final Answer:
{Final Answer: "Circle with center (2,-1) and radius 4"}}

To find the equation of a circle, we need to know its center and radius.
The center of the circle is given as (-2, 5).
The circle passes through the point (1, 9).

\[r = \sqrt{(x_2 - x_1)^2 + (y_2 - y_1)^2}\]
Here, \(x_1\), \(y_1\) are the coordinates of the center (-2, 5) and \(x_2\), \(y_2\) are the coordinates of the point on the circle (1, 9).
\[r = \sqrt{(1 - (-2))^2 + (9 - 5)^2}\]
\[r = \sqrt{3^2 + 4^2}\]
\[r = \sqrt{9 + 16}\]
\[r = \sqrt{25}\]
\[r = 5\]

The equation of a circle with center \(C(x_c, y_c)\) and radius \(r\) is given by:
\[(x - x_c)^2 + (y - y_c)^2 = r^2\]
Substituting the values we have:
\[(x - (-2))^2 + (y - 5)^2 = 5^2\]
\[ (x + 2)^2 + (y - 5)^2 = 25 \]

Given equation: (x - 1)^2 + (y + 2)^2 = 25

This is a circle equation in standard form, which is (x - h)^2 + (y - k)^2 = r^2, where (h, k) represents the center of the circle and 'r' represents its radius.

Center of the circle: (1, -2)
Radius (r): √25 = 5

Therefore, the final answer is:

***********************Done 1{ Final Answer: "y = -x^2 / 4 + 1" }

Explanation:
The general equation for a parabola is y = ax^2 + bx + c.

Since the vertex is at (0, 0), we know that c = 0.

To center the parabola at (0, 4), we can use the equation y - 4 = -(x - 0)^2 / p, where p is a constant. To find p, we can substitute the point (0, 4) into this equation and solve for p:

4 - 4 = -(0)^2 / p
0 = -0 / p

This doesn't give us enough information to determine p, but since we want the parabola to pass through the origin (0, 0), we can substitute x = 0 and y = 0 into the equation:

0 = -(0)^2 / p + c
0 = -0 / p + c

Again, this doesn't give us enough information to determine p. However, since we want the parabola to pass through the point (0, 4), we can substitute x = 0 and y = 4 into the equation:

4 = -(0)^2 / p + c
4 = -0 / p + c

Simplifying this equation gives us c = 4.

Now that we know c = 4, we can rewrite the equation as y - 4 = -(x - 0)^2 / p. To find p, we can substitute x = 1 and y = 3 into this equation:

3 - 4 = -(1 - 0)^2 / p
-1 = -1 / p

Multiplying both sides by p gives us:

-p = -p

Dividing both sides by p (which is nonzero) gives us:

1 = 1

This doesn't give us a value for p, but since we know that the parabola passes through the point (0, 4), we can substitute x = 0 and y = 4 into the equation to get c = 4. We also know that the vertex of the parabola is at (0, 4). The general form for a parabola with its vertex at (h, k) is y - k = a(x - h)^2.

Substituting h = 0 and k = 4 into this equation gives us:

y - 4 = a(x - 0)^2

To find the value of a, we can substitute x = 1 and y = 3 into this equation (since the point (1, 3) lies on the parabola):

3 - 4 = a(1 - 0)^2
-1 = a(1)^2
-1 = a

Substituting a = -1 into the equation gives us:

y - 4 = -(x^2)

Adding 4 to both sides of this equation gives us:

y = -x^2 / 4 + 1

***********************I'm happy to help you solve the problem!

Here's my thought process and solution:

**Step 1: Find the equation of the directrix**

The equation of the directrix is y = 2.

**Step 2: Identify the vertex of the parabola**

The point (3, 4) lies on the parabola. Since it's a parabola with its axis parallel to the x-axis, the vertex is at the midpoint between this point and the focus (which is not given).

However, we can make an assumption that the directrix y = 2 implies that the vertex of the parabola lies on the line y = -1/4*2^2 + c. Since (3,4) lies on the parabola, we can plug in these values and solve for c:

4 = -1/4*2^2 + c
c = 9

So, the vertex is at (5,-1)

**Step 3: Determine the equation of the axis**

Since the directrix has a slope of 0 (y=2), we can assume that the axis of symmetry (which passes through the vertex) also has a slope of 0. Therefore, the equation of the axis is x = 5.

**Step 4: Find the distance between the focus and the vertex**

The focus lies on the line y=2 and the distance between the focus and the vertex can be calculated as:

d^2 = (focus_x - vertex_x)^2 + (focus_y - vertex_y)^2
= (-1 - 5)^2 + (2 - (-1))^2
= 36 + 9
= 45

The distance d is √45.

**Step 5: Construct the equation of the parabola**

Since the axis of symmetry is x = 5, and the vertex is at (5,-1), we can write the equation of the parabola in the form:

y - (-1) = (1/√45)(x - 5)^2

Simplifying this expression gives us:

y + 1 = (1/3√5)(x - 5)^2

Finally, solving for y we get the final answer.

{Final Answer: "y + 1 = (1/3√5)(x - 5)^2"}

  "Explanation": "The given point (2, 3) lies on the parabola and the directrix is x = -1. We know that for any point (x, y) on a parabola with vertex at the origin, the distance from the focus to the point is equal to the perpendicular distance from the point to the directrix.

Since the given point lies on the parabola, the distance between the focus and this point should be equal to the perpendicular distance from the point to the line x = -1. 

Let's denote the equation of the directrix as x = -1 and the coordinates of the focus as (f1,f2). Since the vertex is at the origin, it means that f1=0.

Now let us use the formula for the distance between a point (x,y) and a line Ax+By+C=0 which is |Ax+By+C|/sqrt(A^2+B^2).

For our case A = 1, B = 0, C = 1. 

Using this we get:

Distance from focus to point = sqrt(1^2 + 0^2) * abs(f1 - 2)/|f1-2| = |f1 - 2|

Perpendicular distance from the point (2,3) to x=-1 is also |f1 - 2|

Since both these distances are equal to each other for a parabola with vertex at the origin we have the equation:

|f1 - 2| = |f1 - 2|

We also know that since (2,3) lies on the parabola, then its distance from the focus is equal to y-coordinate of this point. Thus we get another equation f2 = 3.

|f1 - 2| = |f1-2|

This always holds true for any value of f1, and

f2=3.

x = 4/9*(y - 3)^2 + a

The value of 'a' will depend on actual value of f1.
However, we also need to remember that (0,0) is the vertex so the parabola can be written in form of: 

x = A(y-k)^2 + h

In this case:

k = 3 and A=4/9.

So to get the final answer we need find h. But it will be equal to f1 or a, which was any value for our equation. Hence, the parabola can be written as:


x = (4/9)(y-3)^2 + 'a'.



We have already solved this problem with an unknown variable "f1", but we didn't use it in the final answer because we know that the point (0,0) is the vertex. Hence, there is only one possible value of h which will be equal to 2 since (2,3) lies on the parabola and hence its y-coordinate is 3."},

***********************{Explanation:
First, we need to rewrite the given equation x^2 - 4x + 8y + 12 = 0 in standard form for a parabola.

Step 1: Rearrange the terms to group x-terms and y-term together.
x^2 - 4x + 8y = -12

Step 2: Complete the square on the left-hand side of the equation for the x-terms.
(x - 2)^2 = x^2 - 4x + 4
Substituting this back into the equation:
(x - 2)^2 + 8y = -12 + 4

Step 3: Simplify both sides of the equation.
(x - 2)^2 + 8y = -8

Step 4: Solve for y to rewrite the equation in the form of a parabola's vertex.

h = 2, k = -1

***********************{Explanation: The equation of a parabola can be written as y = ax^2 + bx + c, where 'a' is a coefficient that determines the shape and direction of the parabola. 
We are given that the focus of the parabola is at point (2, -1). In a standard parabola with equation y = axx + bx + c, if we know the coordinates of the vertex (h,k) and the focus F(x_0,y_0), then we can use these to find the values of 'a' and 'b'.
For this problem, as we are given the point (2,-1) which is directly above the vertex of the parabola y = ax^2 + bx + c. So, that means h = 1 and k = -4.
The focus F is at a distance of |1/4a| units from the vertex along the axis of symmetry. Since the given point (2,-1) is directly above the vertex, it means the y-coordinate is already known as -1. As it is given that the focus lies on the line y = -4 and has a x coordinate 2 units away from h = 1.
The value of the x coordinate for the focus will be: (h + |(focus_x - h)|) if the focus is to the right of vertex, or else (h - |(focus_x - h)|). Since the point given lies on y = -4 and has an x-coordinate of 2 which is to the left of h = 1. Thus we can say that:
focus_x = h – |focus_x – h|
Substituting values for focus_x
focus_x = 1 – |2-1|
focus_x = 0 
So, x_0 = focus_x = 0 
Now, knowing the coordinates of both vertex (h,k) and focus(x_0,y_0), we can plug them into equations for focus to solve for 'a'.
|y_0 - k| = |1/4a|
|(-4)-(-1)| = |1/(4a)|
3 = |1/(4a)|
If we assume 1/4a is positive
3 = 1 / (4a)
Cross-multiplying and solving for a:
(4a)(3)=1
12a=1
Dividing both sides by 12 to solve for a:
a=1/12
We are given the vertex of the parabola y = ax^2 + bx + c as (h, k) which is (1,-1). We also know that this parabola has its focus on the line y = -4.
Using the standard equation of a parabola y = axx + bx + c, we now have the values for 'a', and 'b' can be any number. However, since no other constraints were provided to find the value of b, it's left as 0 for simplicity. Therefore, our final equation will look like:
y = (1/12)x^2.
Final Answer: "1/12"

x^2 - 4x + y^2 + 2y = 11

Now, we will complete the square for both x and y terms. To do this, we need to add (b/2)^2 to both sides of the equation, where b is the coefficient of the x term (or the y term).

For the x term: -4x + (-4/2)^2 = -4x + 4
For the y term: 2y + (2/2)^2 = 2y + 1

So, the equation becomes:

(x^2 - 4x + 4) + (y^2 + 2y + 1) = 11 + 4 + 1

Simplifying the equation by combining like terms:

(x-2)^2 + (y+1)^2 = 16

This is the standard form of a circle, which is (x-h)^2 + (y-k)^2 = r^2.

Therefore, the center of the circle is at (h, k) = (2, -1), and the radius squared is r^2 = 16.}

***********************{Explanation: To find the points of intersection between the circle x^2 + y^2 = 17 and the line y = x + 3, we need to substitute the expression for y from the equation of the line into the equation of the circle.

Substituting y = x + 3 into the equation x^2 + y^2 = 17 gives:

x^2 + (x + 3)^2 = 17

Expanding and simplifying this equation, we get:

x^2 + x^2 + 6x + 9 = 17
2x^2 + 6x - 8 = 0

Using the quadratic formula to solve for x, we have:

x = (-b ± √(b^2 - 4ac)) / 2a
= (-(6) ± √((6)^2 - 4(2)(-8))) / 2(2)
= (-6 ± √(36 + 64)) / 4
= (-6 ± √100) / 4
= (-6 ± 10) / 4

So, we have two possible values for x:

x = (-6 + 10) / 4 = 1
x = (-6 - 10) / 4 = -4

Now that we have the values of x, we can find the corresponding values of y by substituting them into the equation of the line, y = x + 3.

For x = 1:
y = (1) + 3 = 4

For x = -4:
y = (-4) + 3 = -1

Therefore, the points of intersection between the circle and the line are (1, 4) and (-4, -1).}

  "Explanation": "To find the equation of a hyperbola, we need to use the standard form, which is (x^2/a^2) - (y^2/b^2) = 1 or (y^2/b^2) - (x^2/a^2) = 1. In this case, we are given that the foci are at (±6, 0) and the vertices are at (±4, 0). This means that the transverse axis is along the x-axis.

Since the foci are on the x-axis, the equation will be in the form of (x^2/a^2) - (y^2/b^2) = 1. We know that the distance between each focus and the center is c, which is equal to the distance between the vertices and the center, a. So we have:

c = a

Given the foci are at (±6, 0), we can see that c = 6.

a = √(c^2 - b^2)

We also know that for hyperbola, the relation between a, b, and c is given by: c^2 = a^2 + b^2. We have already found that c = 6.

So, let's solve for 'a' first:

a = √(c^2 - b^2)
a = √(36 - b^2)

We are also given the vertices at (±4, 0). This means a = 4.

Now we can find 'b':

4 = √(36 - b^2)
16 = 36 - b^2
-20 = -b^2
b^2 = 20

So, the equation of hyperbola is:

(x^2/16) - (y^2/20) = 1"

"Final Answer: The final answer is (x^2/16) - (y^2/20) = 1. I hope it is correct."

***********************{ "Explanation": "
પ્રથમ, તમે શિખી ને કે એટલા વિચારને અંકિત કર્યા જણાઈશે કે, \textit{y} = 2x. તેના પછી, આમ,
\begin{align*}
y &amp;= 2x \\
4 &amp;= 2(3) \\
\Rightarrow\qquad y &amp;= \frac{4}{2} = 2
\end{align*}

જ્યારે આ સમીકરણનો વિચલને અંતરનું વ્યાખ્યાન છે. \textbf{y} = 2\textbf{x}. 
\[ y - 4 = -\frac{1}{2}(x-3) \]
\[ (y-4) + \frac{1}{2}(x-3) = 0 \]
તમે અહીં પ્રથમ વાયકાનો સરળીકરણ શુદ્ધ કર્યા જણાઈશે. 
\[ y - 4 + \frac{1}{2}x - \frac{3}{2} = 0 \]
\[\text{Let } x = a, y = b,\text{ and the above equation become } \]

\( b-4+\frac{a}{2}-\frac{3}{2}=0 \)
\[ \Rightarrow b + \frac{1}{2}(a) - \frac{7}{2} = 0 \]
\[ \Rightarrow b + \frac{1}{2} a = \frac{7}{2} \]

એટલે, 
\[ b = \frac{7}{2}-\frac{1}{2}a \]

ત્યારે હુકમ છે,
\[ a=3, b=4 \]
એટલે, \( 4 = \frac{7}{2}-\frac{1}{2}(3) \)

\[ 4 = \frac{7-3}{2} \]

\[ 4 = \frac{4}{2} \]

\( 4 = 2 \), જો તમારે નીચે આપેલો વિભાગ શ્રોતિયું કરો, અને દાખલાઓ જોઈએ.
\[ x = a, y=b \]
\[ x=3, y=4 \]

આપણે \( 2x \) ક્રમેની ગુણાત્મક ફંક્શન સિવાય, \textit{y} = 2\textbf{x}, એટલે \( x=3, y=4 \) જોઈએ.

\[ (x, y)= (a,b) = (3, 4) \]

ત્યારે આપણે \( x = a, y=b \), અને, \( x=3, y=4. \)
\[ b + \frac{1}{2}a = \frac{7}{2} \]

તમે બિંદુ (x,y) જોઈએ અને \( a=3, b=4. \)
\[ b + \frac{1}{2}a = 4 +\frac{1}{2}(3) \]

ત્યારે હુકમ છે,
\[ 4+\frac{1}{2}\times 3=7 \]
\[ b=\frac{7}{2}-\frac{a}{2} = 4 + \frac{1}{2}(3) -\frac{1}{2}x = 4 + \frac{3}{2} - \frac{x}{2} \]

અને,
\[ b=4+\frac{3- x}{2}=4+\frac{-1}{2} = \frac{7-x}{2} \]
જો તમે કે, એટલા \( y= 2x, (3, 4) , \)
\[ b = \frac{7-x}{2}=4 \]

તમે \( x=3 \), જોઈએ
\[ 4= \frac{7-3}{2}=\frac{4}{2} \]
અન્ય કહીએ, 
\[ b = 2 \]

\textbf{y} = 2\textbf{x}
આમ,
\[ y= 2x = 2(3) = 6 \]

તેથી \( x = a, y=b \)
\[ (a,b) = (3,6) \]
\( b + \frac{1}{2}a = \frac{7}{2} \)

\textbf{(x,y)=(a,b)= (3,4)}, અને 
$y= 2x $,

\[ b+\frac{1}{2} a = \frac{7}{2} \]

ત્યારે હુકમ છે,
\[ 4 +\frac{3- x}{2}= \frac{-x+7}{2} = \frac{7-x}{2} = 4 \]
\[ -x+7=8 \]

\[ (-x) + (7)=8 \]

\[ -x+7 = 8 \]

\[ -x=1 \]

અને,
\[ x = -a \]

\[ a=-(-x) \]
\( a = -1 \)
ત્યારે \( x = -1, y=2(-1)=4-2\), જોઈએ
\[ 2x = 4 - 2 = 2 \]

આ પછી, 
\[ b+\frac{a}{2}=\frac{7}{2} \]
અને,
\[ a=-(-1)=+1, b=4-2=2 \]

ત્યારે \( x = 3, y = 4 \),
\[ b + \frac{a}{2}=4+\frac{(3)}{2}=\frac{7}{2}-\frac{x}{2} = \frac{7-x}{2} \]
\[ b+\frac{1}{2}(x)=2+\frac{1}{2}\times(3) = 2 + \frac{3}{2} - \frac{x}{2} \]

તેના પછી, 
\[ x=3,y=4,b=\frac{7-x}{2},a=x=-(-x)=1 \]

\[ b = 2+\frac{3- x}{2}= 2+ \frac{-1}{2} = 2 + \frac{3 - (-1)}{2} = 2 + \frac{(4 - x)}{2} = 2 +\frac{(x-1)x}{2} \]
\[ b+\frac{a}{2}=\frac{7-x}{2} = 4 + \frac{3}{2}-\frac{x}{2}=2+ \frac{-1+x}{2} = 2+\frac{(3 - x)}{2} = 2+\frac{(x-1)x}{2}\]

\[ b= 2 + \frac{(-1+x)(4-x)}{2} = 2 +\frac{(-1+x)\times (1+ (-3)+x) }{2}=2+ \frac{((-1)^2 - x\times(-3)-3\times(-1)x)}{2}\]

\[ b=2+\frac{((x-1)(4-x))}{2} = 2 +\frac{(x^2-4x+x-1)}{2}= \frac{-7+4x-x^2}{2} = \frac{x(3x-5) - (-7)}{2}\]

\[ b= 2 + \frac{(-7+ x\times (3x-5)) }{2}\]
\[ b=\frac{(4x-(x)^2)(1)+7}{2} =\frac{((x-7)\times(x-1)x) }{2}\]
અને,

***********************{Final Answer: y = 1/4x^2 - 2}

Explanation:

To find the equation of a parabola that opens to the left with vertex (-4, 1), we need to use the vertex form of a parabolic equation.

The general vertex form is:
y = a(x-h)^2 + k
where (h,k) is the vertex. In this case, h = -4 and k = 1.

Since the parabola opens left, the coefficient a must be negative. We can also see that the parabola passes through the point (-2,1), so we can use this point to find the value of 'a'.

Let's substitute x=-2 and y=1 into the equation:
1 = a(-2-(-4))^2 + 1
Simplifying:
1 = a(2)^2 + 1
0 = 4a (since the constants cancel out)
-a = 0

This gives us 'a' as -1/4.

So, substituting a=-1/4 and h=k=-4 into the vertex form equation, we get:

y = (-1/4)(x-(-4))^2 + 1
Simplifying:
y = (-1/4)(x+4)^2 + 1
Expanding:
y = -1/4(x^2 + 8x + 16) + 1
Multiplying the x-term by the coefficient:
y = -1/4x^2 - 2x - 4 + 1

Combining like terms:
y = -1/4x^2 - 2x -3

***********************{Explanation:
A circle's equation is given by (x - h)^2 + (y - k)^2 = r^2, where (h,k) is the center and r is the radius. In this case, the center of the circle is on the y-axis (i.e., its x-coordinate is 0). So, we can set h = 0 in the equation. The point (0,5) lies on the circle, so plugging it into the equation will give us the value of r.

(0 - 0)^2 + (5 - k)^2 = r^2
25 - 10k + k^2 = r^2

Since this circle passes through the point (0,5), we can plug in these coordinates for x and y:

r^2 = 25

Now, let's take the square root of both sides to solve for r:

r = √25
r = 5

Now that we have the value of r, we can write the equation of the circle.

(x - h)^2 + (y - k)^2 = r^2

We already know that h = 0 and r = 5. Plugging these values into the equation gives us:

x^2 + (y - 5)^2 = 25

So, the equation of the circle is x^2 + (y - 5)^2 = 25.

  "Explanation": "To find the equation of a circle passing through points (1, 1), (2, -3), and (5, -1), we can follow these steps:

1. First, let's assume the center of the circle is at point (h, k).

The general equation of a circle with center (h, k) and radius r is given by:
(x - h)^2 + (y - k)^2 = r^2

We know that this circle passes through points (1, 1), (2, -3), and (5, -1). We can substitute these coordinates into the equation:

(1 - h)^2 + (1 - k)^2 = r^2
(2 - h)^2 + (-3 - k)^2 = r^2
(5 - h)^2 + (-1 - k)^2 = r^2

Now, we can solve for h and k by equating the first two equations:

(1 - h)^2 + (1 - k)^2 = (2 - h)^2 + (-3 - k)^2
We can expand and simplify this equation to find the values of h and k.

Let's do it step-by-step:

1. Expand both sides of the equation:
1 - 2h + h^2 + 1 - 2k + k^2 = 4 - 4h + h^2 + 9 + 6k + k^2
Combine like terms:

-2h - 2k + 2 = 13 - 4h + 6k

2. Move all the h and k terms to one side of the equation:
2h + 2k + 15 = 0
Divide both sides by 2:
h + k + 7.5 = 0

Now, let's find the value of k using this information.

Using the first equation, we can substitute h = -7.5 - k into it:

(1 - (-7.5 - k))^2 + (1 - k)^2 = r^2
Expand and simplify:
(8.5 + k)^2 + (1 - k)^2 = r^2

Let's use the fact that this circle also passes through point (5, -1):

(5 - (-7.5 - k))^2 + (-1 - k)^2 = r^2
Expand and simplify:
(12.5 + k)^2 + (-1 - k)^2 = r^2

Now, we can equate the first two equations to find k:

(8.5 + k)^2 + (1 - k)^2 = (12.5 + k)^2 + (-1 - k)^2
Expand both sides:
89.25 + 17k + k^2 + 1 - 2k + k^2 = 156.25 + 25k + k^2 + 1 + 2k + k^2

Simplify and combine like terms:

-8.25 + 15k = 156.25
Add 8.25 to both sides:
15k = 164.5
Divide by 15:
k = 11

Now, we can find the value of h using h + k + 7.5 = 0:

h + 11 + 7.5 = 0
Subtract 11 and 7.5 from both sides:
h = -18.5

So, the center of the circle is at point (h, k) = (-18.5, 11).

We can now find the radius r using one of the equations.

Let's use (1 - h)^2 + (1 - k)^2 = r^2:
(1 + 18.5)^2 + (1 - 11)^2 = r^2
Expand and simplify:
193.25 + (-10)^2 = r^2
193.25 + 100 = r^2
293.25 = r^2

Finally, we can find the equation of the circle:

(x + 18.5)^2 + (y - 11)^2 = 293.25"

}

***********************{Explanation:
The given equation is a hyperbola, and we can identify its key components by comparing it to the standard form of a hyperbola:

x^2/a^2 - y^2/b^2 = 1
In this case, we have:
a^2/4 - b^2/9 = 1

We can rewrite the equation as:
x^2/4 - y^2/9 = 1

Comparing this with the standard form, we get a^2 = 4 and b^2 = 9.

To find the foci of the hyperbola, we need to calculate c using the formula:
c^2 = a^2 + b^2
Plugging in the values, we get:
c^2 = 4 + 9
= 13

Taking the square root of both sides gives us:
c = √13

The foci of the hyperbola are located on the x-axis at points (±c, 0), which is (±√13, 0) in this case.

To find the vertices of the hyperbola, we need to calculate a using the formula:
a = √(a^2)
Plugging in the value, we get:
a = √4
= 2

The vertices are located on the x-axis at points (±a, 0), which is (±2, 0) in this case.

Finally, to find the asymptotes of the hyperbola, we can use the formula:
y = ±(b/a)x
Plugging in the values, we get:
y = ±(3/2)x

Therefore, the foci of the hyperbola are (±√13, 0), the vertices are (±2, 0), and the asymptotes are y = ±(3/2)x.}

{Final Answer: "√13"}
